[{"id":51831,"date":"2026-04-16T15:02:46","date_gmt":"2026-04-16T19:02:46","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/?p=51831"},"modified":"2026-04-23T08:37:07","modified_gmt":"2026-04-23T12:37:07","slug":"arc-seeking-applicants-for-leadership-development-program-focused-on-multi-sector-economic-development-2","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/arc-seeking-applicants-for-leadership-development-program-focused-on-multi-sector-economic-development-2\/","title":{"rendered":"ARC\u00a0Seeking Applicants for\u00a0Leadership\u00a0Development Program Focused on Multi-Sector Economic Development"},"content":{"rendered":" \n  Public, private and nonprofit leaders throughout Appalachia are encouraged to apply for ARC\u2019s free eight-month training program.   \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C.,\u00a0April\u00a016, 2026 \u00a0\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) opened applications for the\u00a0eighth\u00a0class of the Appalachian Leadership Institute (ALI),\u00a0an eight month leadership development training opportunity with a focus on economic development in Appalachia. Anyone living or working in Appalachian’s 423 counties is eligible to apply. \n \n\n \n The program, at no cost to participants, focuses on skill-building, mentoring and collaboration to grow Appalachia’s economic feature. The curriculum comprises six multi-day seminars across Appalachia, each focusing on one of ARC’s investment priorities, and concludes with a graduation ceremony in Binghamton, New York. \n \n\n \n From October 2026 to June 2027, the eighth cohort of fellows will learn to:  \n \n\n \n \n Recognize and utilize unique assets in their communities to build economic development plans.  \n \n \n\n \n \n Identify and implement strategies to strengthen their communities through improved infrastructure, business development, workforce development, tourism and community skill-building.  \n \n \n\n \n \n Collaborate with a\u202fnetwork of leaders across Appalachia on strategies to expand the region\u2019s future growth.  \n \n \n\n \n \u201cBy bringing together changemakers from across our 13 states, ARC\u2019s Appalachian Leadership Institute not only invests in the future of our Appalachian economy, but also in the future of our Appalachian people,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin.  \u201cI\u2019m confident the members of our eighth cohort will continue the institute\u2019s work of learning and sharing by using collaboration as a tool to help our region reach its full economic potential, and I look forward to seeing the applications we receive.\u201d  \n \n\n \n The Appalachian Leadership Institute is limited to 40 fellows each year and includes participants from each of the 13 Appalachian states. Applicants may be from the public, private or non-profit sectors, and each class represents a wide network of experiences.   \n \n\n \n “We’ve made it clear in Mississippi and the Appalachian Region that we are going to compete and win when it comes to economic growth and opportunity. That takes leadership,\u201d said  ARC 2026 States\u2019 Co-Chair, Mississippi Governor Tate Reeves . \u201cThe Appalachian Leadership Institute is helping build that leadership pipeline across our state and region. I encourage Appalachians who are ready to step up and make an impact to apply and be part of that effort.”  \n \n\n \n Upon completion of the program, fellows automatically join the ALI Alumni Network, a peer-to-peer working group committed to collaborative solutions that strengthen Appalachia. The alumni network provides an opportunity to stay connected to the network of more than 200 ALI alumni and to continue learning from leaders in Appalachian economic development.  \n \n\n \n Applications for the 2026-2027 class are open through June 1, 2026. To learn more about the program and apply, visit:  arc.gov\/leadership .   \n \n\n \n ###  \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Leadership Institute
The Appalachian Leadership Institute is a comprehensive regional leadership training program developed by the Appalachian Regional Commission in partnership with the University of Tennessee, Knoxville; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact. The current class of the Appalachian Leadership Institute includes representatives from a variety of sectors and industries such as education, healthcare, economic development, public service, and other professions.\u00a0 \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to invest and partner to strengthen economic development in Appalachia to help the region reach its full economic potential.  \n ","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":" Public, private and nonprofit leaders throughout Appalachia are encouraged to apply for ARC\u2019s free eight-month training program.  WASHINGTON, D.C.,\u00a0April\u00a016, 2026\u00a0\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) opened applications for the\u00a0eighth\u00a0class of the Appalachian Leadership Institute (ALI),\u00a0an eight month leadership development training opportunity with a focus on economic development in Appalachia. Announces Appalachian Leadership Institute Class of 2025-2026\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":" \n   New class of ARC\u2019s economic development training program comprises 40 fellows from across all 13 Appalachian states.    \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C., October 23, 2025 \u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced the 40 fellows selected for the 2025-2026 class of the  Appalachian Leadership Institute  (ALI), a training opportunity that equips  Appalachian  community leaders to use economic development as a tool to drive positive change. The seventh ALI class comprises a network of public, private and nonprofit leaders from all 13 Appalachian states.  \n \n\n \n The eight-month program, which kicked off with an orientation session in Ashland, KY, this week, is anchored by six multi-day seminars across the Appalachian Region. Each seminar focuses on one of ARC\u2019s  strategic investment priorities  and will prepare these fellows to build effective economic and community development strategies, integrate their local assets into wider regional development strategies and use new connections to forge strong regional partnerships.  \n \n\n \n \u201cInvesting in Appalachia\u2019s local leaders is an integral part of maintaining a strong economy for our region,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cI\u2019m proud of the way ARC\u2019s Appalachian Leadership Institute capitalizes on the talent already within our 13 states and empowers fellows to connect and collaborate on projects that will impact their communities and Appalachia as a region.\u201d  \n \n\n \n The  2025-2026 Class of ALI fellows  were selected via a competitive application process to reflect the region\u2019s wide range of economic development challenges, opportunities and strategies. The class includes:  \n \n\n \n \n  Alabama  \n \n Christy Atkinson, Executive Director, Workshops Empowerment, Inc., Pell City  \n\n\n\n Jori Chatman, Program Manager of Government & Community Relations, Tennessee Valley Authority, Florence  \n\n\n\n Mary-Marshall VanSant, Director of Center for Learning & Professional Development, University of North Alabama, Tuscumbia  \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Georgia  \n \n Jessica Dudley, President, United Way of Hall County, Lula  \n\n\n\n Noel Durant, AVP Tennessee State Director, Trust for Public Land, Chattanooga  \n\n\n\n Alexander Harrison, Director of Parks & Recreation, Catoosa County GA, Ringgold \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Kentucky  \n \n Rachel Chambers, Supervisory Community Liaison, USDA Rural Development, Jackson  \n\n\n\n Gene Detherage, Jr., Recovery Support Specialist, Bluegrass Area Development District, Morehead  \n\n\n\n Tyler Stapp, Chief Operating Officer, Pineville Community Health Center, Russell Springs  \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Maryland  \n \n Connor Norman, Business Development Specialist, Garrett County Government, Oakland \n\n\n\n Melissa Clark, Associate Director, AHEC West, Fort Ashby \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Mississippi  \n \n Abbie Bell, Project Manager, Three Rivers Planning & Development District, Pototoc  \n\n\n\n Blake Neely, Field Representative, U.S. Senator Cindy Hyde-Smith, Oxford  \n\n\n\n Philip Shackelford, Executive Director & CEO, Lee-Itawamba Library System, Guntown  \n \n  \n\n\n\n  New York  \n \n Robin Alpaugh, Certified Business Advisor, Southern Tier Small Business Development Center, Binghamton  \n\n\n\n Caitlin Croft, Executive Director, NYS Ski Racing Association, Little Valley  \n\n\n\n Kimberly Ecklund, Mayor, City of Jamestown, Jamestown  \n\n\n\n Kasie Foulk, Deputy Director of Housing Policy & Development, Jamestown Urban Renewal Agency, Jamestown  \n \n  \n\n\n\n  North Carolina  \n \n Jazmyne Baylor, Research & Instruction Librarian, Western Carolina University, Cullowhee  \n\n\n\n Becky The Appalachian Leadership Institute is a comprehensive economic development training program for regional leaders developed by the Appalachian Regional Commission in partnership with the University of Tennessee, Knoxville; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact, LLC. Learn more at www.arc.gov\/leadership.    \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.  \n .org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}},{"id":50050,"date":"2025-09-17T11:07:45","date_gmt":"2025-09-17T15:07:45","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/?p=50050"},"modified":"2025-09-30T07:34:37","modified_gmt":"2025-09-30T11:34:37","slug":"arc-awards-11-million-to-32-projects-supporting-appalachians-in-recovery-from-substance-use-disorder","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/arc-awards-11-million-to-32-projects-supporting-appalachians-in-recovery-from-substance-use-disorder\/","title":{"rendered":"ARC Awards $11 Million to 32 Projects Supporting Appalachians in Recovery from Substance Use Disorder\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":" \n  Through ARC\u2019s INSPIRE Initiative, the award package will expand support services to help Appalachians in recovery enter and re-enter the workforce in nine Appalachian states.    \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, DC, September 23, 2025 \u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) awarded $11 million to 32 projects through its \u202f Investments Supporting Partnerships in Recovery Ecosystems (INSPIRE)  \u202fInitiative, which aims to address workforce gaps and economic challenges due to the impact of substance use disorder (SUD) in Appalachia.    \n \n\n \n   ARC\u2019s 2025 INSPIRE grantees   will strengthen the SUD recovery ecosystem in 165 counties across nine Appalachian states\u2014 Georgia, Kentucky, New York, North Carolina, Ohio, Pennsylvania, Tennessee, Virginia and West Virginia \u2014by providing community support to help individuals in SUD recovery enter or re-enter the workforce while maintaining their recovery. Twenty of the impacted counties are first-time INSPIRE award recipients.   \n \n\n \n \u201cIn order to have a healthy Appalachian economy, we must make sure we have a healthy Appalachian workforce,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cI am so proud of the way ARC\u2019s INSPIRE initiative ecosystem empowers Appalachians in substance use disorder recovery to gain the training, resources and support they need to enter or re-enter the workforce while also becoming productive members of their families and communities.\u201d  \n \n\n \n “Everyone knows someone affected by substance use. It will take all of us working together to get those in recovery back on their feet,” said  ARC 2025 States’ Co-Chair, Maryland Governor Wes Moore.  “Today’s announcement accelerates our work to help those in need, while also building fresh pathwas to work, wages and wealth for all. This is good news for public health, good news for economic growth, and good news for mounting prosperity across the region.” \n \n\n \n Since April 2021, ARC\u2019s INSPIRE Initiative has invested $65.7 million in 200 projects that have impacted 380 counties across all 13 Appalachian states. These investments have secured an additional $48.1 million in matching project funds and are projected to improve nearly 4,000 businesses and help prepare over 18,100 individuals for new job opportunities.  \n \n\n \n   New Data Reveals Reduction in SUD-related Deaths in Appalachia
SUD recovery-to-work initiatives may be starting to make an impact on overdose mortality, according to a  new ARC research report  that examines trends in diseases of despair including overdose, suicide, and liver disease. From 2021 to 2023, the overdose mortality rate decreased 10 percent in the Appalachian Region. However, work remains to be done, as these rates\u2014in both Appalachia and the rest of the country\u2014remain much higher than the pre-pandemic figures of 2019.  \n \n\n \n Learn more about ARC\u2019s INSPIRE Initiative and our new INSPIRE grantees at\u202f arc.gov\/SUD .  \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.  \n Announces Appalachian Leadership Institute Class of 2024-2025\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":" \n   Sixth class of ARC\u2019s nine-month economic development training program comprises 40 fellows from the public, private, and nonprofit sectors across all 13 Appalachian states.   \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C., October 23, 2024 \u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced the 40 fellows selected for the 2024-2025 Class of the  Appalachian Leadership Institute  (ALI), a leadership training opportunity that equips  Appalachian  community leaders to use economic development as a tool to drive positive change. The sixth ALI class comprises a diverse network of public, private, and nonprofit leaders from all 13 Appalachian states. \n \n\n \n The nine-month curriculum, which kicked off with an orientation session in Knoxville, TN this week, is anchored by six multi-day seminars across the Appalachian Region. Each seminar focuses on one of ARC\u2019s  strategic investment priorities  and includes skill-building workshops, best practice analysis, networking, and site visits to see ARC  investments in action . \n \n\n \n \u201cThe Appalachian Leadership Institute supports the enduring work of Appalachia\u2019s changemakers, while helping them advance their skillsets and build upon   valuable regional connections with likeminded leaders,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cI send my sincerest congratulations to our newest round of ALI fellows knowing their passion and purpose will ensure an even more vibrant future for Appalachia\u2019s communities.\u201d \n \n\n \n The  2024-2025 Class of ALI fellows  \u2013 who were selected via a competitive application process to reflect the Appalachian region\u2019s wide range of economic development challenges, opportunities, and strategies \u2013 includes: \n \n\n \n \n  Alabama  \n \n  Ryan Cate Gibson , CEO, Holistic Performance Group, Decatur \n\n\n\n  Paul DeMarco , Partner, Parsons, Lee & Juliano, P.C., Vestavia Hills \n\n\n\n  Eric Wright , Director of Planning and Development, East Alabama Regional Planning and Development Commission, Anniston \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Georgia  \n \n  Adrienne Cloud , CEO, SMARTech Construction, Flowery Branch \n\n\n\n  Andrew Davenport , Founder, A New Veteran, Gainesville \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Kentucky  \n \n  Casey Cash , Infrastructure Development Coordinator, Bluegrass Area Development District, Lexington \n\n\n\n  Colby Hall , Executive Director, Shaping Our Appalachian Region (SOAR), Pikeville \n\n\n\n  Caitlin Mason , Assistant Director of Economic Development, Cumberland Valley Area Development District, London \n\n\n\n  Jessica Miles , Membership & Grants Administrator, Appalachia Funders Network, Frenchburg \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Maryland  \n \n  Delanie Blubaugh , Program Manager, The Greater Cumberland Committee, Cumberland \n\n\n\n  Annaston Cree , Western Maryland Field Representative, U.S. Congressman David Trone MD-06, Frederick \n\n\n\n  Kevin Simmers , Founder\/Executive Director, Brooke\u2019s House, Hagerstown \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Mississippi Grants Specialist, Cherokee County Local Government, Murphy \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Ohio  \n \n  Eli Cole , Development Specialist, Ohio Valley Regional Development Commission, Waverly \n\n\n\n  Tara Gilts , Philanthropy Advisor, OhioHealth Foundation, Athens \n\n\n\n  Chandler Grooms , Tourism Development Director, Pike County Chamber of Commerce & Tourism, Waverly \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Pennsylvania  \n \n Greenville \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Tennessee  \n \n  Heath Guinn , Founder, Sync Space, Kingsport \n\n\n\n  Tammy O\u2019Dell , Director of Grants, Motlow State Community College, Lynchburg \n\n\n\n  Lottie Ryans , Director of Workforce and Literacy Initiatives, First Tennessee Development District, Johnson City \n \n  \n\n\n\n  Virginia  \n \n  Mandy Archer , Program Administrator, Virginia Department of Housing & Community Development Enterprise Zone Program, Galax \n\n\n\n  Dana Cronkhite , Director of Economic Development, Dickenson County, Clintwood \n\n\n\n  Thomas Lawson , Regional Planner, LENOWISCO Planning District Commission, Duffield \n \n  \n\n\n\n  West Virginia  \n \n  Maverly Davis , Director of Lending, New Economy Works WV, Charleston \n\n\n\n  Heather Foster , Chief Impact Officer, AmeriCorps Mid-Atlantic, Charleston \n\n\n\n  Katie Sams , Director of Business Development, WVU Medicine Camden Clark Medical Center, Parkersburg \n\n\n\n  Liz Vitullo , Assistant Vice President of Economic Innovation, West Virginia University, Morgantown \n \n  \n \n \n\n \n The program will prepare these fellows to build effective economic and community development strategies, integrate their local assets into wider regional development strategies, identify public grants and other community development resources, and use new connections to forge strong regional partnerships. \n \n\n \n \u201cARC\u2019s Appalachian Leadership Institute is a valuable program that allows participants to learn more about each of ARC\u2019s strategic investment priorities and the needs of the region,\u201d said  ARC States\u2019 Co-Chair Tennessee Governor Bill Lee.  \u201cI\u2019m proud of the three Tennesseans chosen to participate in this class and look forward to seeing how they’ll use their leadership skills to engage in their communities.\u201d \n \n\n \n Upon completion of the program, fellows will continue to build and share knowledge with hundreds of other leaders in the Appalachian Leadership Institute Alumni Network, a peer working group fostering collaborative solutions to build a stronger future for Appalachia. \n \n\n \n To learn more about the program, visit:  arc.gov\/leadership . To meet the 2024-2025 class of fellows and learn more about their work to strengthen Appalachian communities, visit:  https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/academies-and-institutes\/leadership\/class-of-2024-2025\/ . \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Leadership Institute
The Appalachian Leadership Institute is a comprehensive regional leadership training program developed by the Appalachian Regional Commission in partnership with the University of Tennessee, Knoxville; The Howard H. Baker School of Public Policy and Public Affairs; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact. Learn more at www.arc.gov\/leadership. \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. \n ","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":" Sixth class of ARC\u2019s nine-month economic development training program comprises 40 fellows from the public, private, and nonprofit sectors across all 13 Appalachian states. Awards $68.2 Million for Economic Revitalization in Appalachia\u2019s Coal-Impacted Communities"},"content":{"rendered":" \n  Largest POWER funding package to date is expected to create more than 2,400 new jobs and train over 10,500 workers for new opportunities in Appalachia\u2019s coal-impacted communities across 10 states .
\n \n\n \n  PITTSBURGH, Pa., October 16, 2024 \u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) awarded  $68.2 million to 65 projects  through its  Partnerships for Opportunity and Workforce and Economic Revitalization (POWER) Initiative . POWER directs federal resources to economic diversification and revitalization projects in Appalachian communities affected by the downturn of the coal industry. \n \n\n \n Today\u2019s award package is ARC’s largest POWER investment since the initiative was launched in 2015, with projects impacting 188 counties in 10 Appalachian states: Alabama, Kentucky, Mississippi, New York, North Carolina, Ohio, Pennsylvania, Tennessee, Virginia and West Virginia. ARC recognizes that Hurricane Helene has impacted multiple Appalachian communities and grantees, including some POWER award recipients. ARC will continue working with affected state and grantee partners to address their needs as they recover and rebuild. \n \n\n \n \u201cARC\u2019s POWER initiative supports coal-impacted communities’ preparation for the next phase of Appalachia\u2019s economy, while ensuring that residents have a say in the course of their own futures,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cThe investments announced in this round of POWER will help train workers, advance new industries, and build upon the progress already being made toward a brighter future full of economic opportunity for our region.\u201d  \n \n\n \n ARC\u2019s 2024 POWER awardees and their partners will use funding to strengthen a variety of industries \u2013 including advanced manufacturing, entrepreneurship, healthcare, and workforce development \u2013 to enhance job training and employment opportunities, create jobs in existing and new industries, and attract new sources of private investment in coal-impacted communities.  \n \n\n \n ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin announced the awards alongside state and federal partners and grantees at the Mill 19 in Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania, headquarters of POWER grantee  Catalyst Connection  and sub-grantees Carnegie Mellon University\u2019s Manufacturing Futures Institute and the Advanced Robotics for Manufacturing (ARM) Institute. Catalyst Connection\u2019s CEO Petra Mitchell spoke about the new $2 million POWER grant to help create pathways to employment in advanced manufacturing.\u00a0 \n \n\n \n \u201cWe are excited for our new POWER project, which will allow us to build on our progress of preparing our regional workforce for in-demand jobs in the advanced manufacturing industry,” said  Catalyst Connection President and CEO Petra Mitchell.  “Manufacturing jobs offer workers from hard hit Appalachian communities hope and opportunities for family-sustaining wages and community development. When manufacturing companies and workers succeed, everyone in the local community can benefit.” \n \n\n \n Since 2015, ARC has invested $484.7 million in 564 projects impacting 365 coal-impacted counties. Collectively, these investments are projected to support nearly 54,000 jobs and prepare nearly 170,000 workers and students for new opportunities in growing industries.  \n \n\n \n ARC plans to release a notice of solicitations of applications (NOSA) for the POWER Initiative in early 2025. \n \n\n \n Learn more about ARC\u2019s POWER Initiative and our new POWER grantees at  arc.gov\/POWER . \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. \n Awards Nearly $11.5 Million to 39 Projects Supporting Appalachians in Recovery from Substance Use Disorder"},"content":{"rendered":" \n  Through ARC\u2019s INSPIRE Initiative, the award package will expand support services to help Appalachians in recovery enter and re-enter the workforce in nine Appalachian states.  \n \n\n \n  Asheville, N.C., September 18, 2024 \u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) awarded nearly  $11.5 million to 39 projects  through its  Investments Supporting Partnerships in Recovery Ecosystems (INSPIRE)  Initiative, which aims to address the impact of substance use disorder (SUD) in Appalachia with investments in projects that create or expand services in the recovery ecosystem leading to workforce entry and re-entry. \n \n\n \n  ARC\u2019s 2024 INSPIRE grantees  will strengthen the SUD recovery ecosystem in 127 counties in nine Appalachian states\u2014Kentucky, New York, North Carolina, Ohio, Pennsylvania, South Carolina, Tennessee, Virginia, and West Virginia\u2014by expanding recovery-focused partnerships, peer support and other wraparound services, and skills and workforce training programs that help prepare individuals in recovery for meaningful job opportunities. \n \n\n \n \u201cSubstance use disorder is a region-wide epidemic that impacts Appalachian families and community workforces,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cI commend our 2024 INSPIRE grantees for their dedication in helping Appalachians who have struggled with substance use disorder regain a sense of hope and purpose by enabling them to rejoin their communities, bolster workforce development, and make positive impacts on the region.\u201d \n \n\n \n “In order to truly address substance use disorder, we must create an environment that includes support for wrap-around services such as housing, job training, and counseling,” said  ARC States’ Co-Chair Tennessee Governor Bill Lee.  “INSPIRE funding helps make this possible by empowering organizations to make an impact across the Appalachian region. We are proud of Tennessee’s nine grantees who are focused on this important work.” \n \n\n \n Federal Co-Chair Manchin made the award announcement with  North Carolina Health and Human Services Secretary Kody Kinsley , grantees, and state partners during a press conference at the headquarters of  Land of Sky Regional Council \u2014INSPIRE grantee and local development district\u2014in Asheville, North Carolina. \n \n\n \n \u201cCommunities in the Appalachian region, especially rural towns, have borne the brunt of the disease of addiction,\u201d said  Secretary Kinsley . \u201cWe are grateful to our federal partners for tackling these disparities head-on by investing in behavioral health services and supports.\u201d \n \n\n \n With this award package, ARC has invested $53.6 million in 166 projects across all 13 Appalachian states since INSPIRE was established in April of 2021. Together, ARC\u2019s INSPIRE investments will impact 360 Appalachian counties, improve over 3,000 businesses and help prepare nearly 16,200 individuals for new opportunities in the workforce. \n \n\n \n  New Data Reveals Reduction in SUD-related Deaths in Appalachia
SUD recovery-to-work initiatives may be starting to make an impact, according to a  new ARC research report  that examines trends in diseases of despair including overdose, suicide, and liver disease. From 2021 to 2022, the overall diseases of despair mortality rate decreased 4 percent in the Appalachian Region while remaining virtually unchanged in the rest of the United States. However, work remains to be done, as these rates\u2014in both Appalachia and the rest of the country\u2014remain much higher than the pre-pandemic figures of 2019. \n \n\n \n ARC expects to issue a Notice of Solicitation for Applications (NOSA) for the next round of INSPIRE funding in early 2025. \n \n\n \n Learn more about ARC’s INSPIRE Initiative and our new INSPIRE grantees at  arc.gov\/SUD . \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. \n Administration Through ARC Awards $16.9 Million to Grow Appalachia\u2019s Infrastructure, Businesses, Workforce and Tourism Across 10 States"},"content":{"rendered":" \n  Five new Appalachian Regional Commission ARISE grants drive multi-state collaboration to transform the regional economy.  \n \n\n \n  Chattanooga, Tenn., September 5, 2024 \u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced $16.9 million in funding for five collaborative projects to expand the region\u2019s infrastructure, tourism, business development and workforce opportunities. Funded through ARC\u2019s  Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE) , these projects bring together more than 60 partners representing at least 270 counties across 10 Appalachian states\u2014including one of Appalachia\u2019s federally recognized  Tribal Communities \u2014to drive large-scale economic transformation. ARISE grantees announced today include: \n \n\n \n \n   $8,000,000 to Save the Children Federation, Inc.   to grow the region\u2019s childcare and early education workforce in Kentucky, Tennessee and West Virginia through training, credentialing, apprenticeships and technical assistance. \n\n\n\n   $4,500,000 to the Beneficial Electrification League   to modernize electric utility grid infrastructure in Alabama, Georgia, Mississippi, North Carolina, New York, Ohio, Pennsylvania and Tennessee. \n\n\n\n   $3,889,964 to the Appalachian Investors Alliance   to establish micro-venture and angel funds for entrepreneurs and small businesses, and launch the Appalachian Venture Foundry to prepare businesses for investments, in Kentucky, Mississippi, Ohio, Pennsylvania, Tennessee and West Virginia. \n\n\n\n   $500,000 to Carnegie Mellon University   to plan for the expansion of job skills matching and career support services in Pennsylvania and West Virginia. \n\n\n\n   $72,219 to the Pennsylvania Environmental Council   to plan for improved trail connectivity to boost tourism in Pennsylvania and New York, including the Seneca Nation of Indians. \n \n \n\n \n Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin announced the awards during ARC\u2019s  2024 Annual Conference  in Chattanooga, Tennessee. She was joined by ARC\u2019s 2024 States\u2019 Co-Chair Governor Bill Lee and hundreds of federal, state and community leaders from across the 13-state Appalachian Region. \n \n\n \n \u201cBy combining multi-state collaboration with innovative partnerships, ARC\u2019s ARISE projects serve as models for how we can work as one, united Appalachia to maximize economic growth,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cI congratulate our newest ARISE grantees and am proud to have announced them in the presence of many of our federal, state, and local partners at ARC\u2019s annual conference. I have no doubt that these ARISE projects\u2014which include both planning and implementation grants\u2014will build on previous successes and result in greater opportunities for our Appalachian people and communities.\u201d \n \n\n \n With today\u2019s package, ARC has invested  $88.2 million  in  34 ARISE projects  to support the development of new economic opportunities  across all 13 Appalachian states . \n \n\n \n Made possible through support from the Bipartisan Infrastructure Law, ARISE drives large-scale, regional economic transformation through collaborative multi-state projects. \n \n\n \n To learn more about the new ARISE grantees, visit  www.arc.gov\/grants-and-opportunities\/arise\/arise-project-summaries\/ . \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. \n ","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":" Five new Appalachian Regional Commission ARISE grants drive multi-state collaboration to transform the regional economy. Chattanooga, Tenn., September 5, 2024\u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced $16.9 million in funding for five collaborative projects to expand the region\u2019s infrastructure, tourism, business development and workforce opportunities. Funded through ARC\u2019s Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE), […] Regional Commission Selects 30 Community Foundations for No-Cost Training Program and Funding Opportunity"},"content":{"rendered":" \n  READY Community Foundations will provide 30 community foundations serving the Appalachian Region with no-cost training and access to funding to expand local philanthropic impact.  \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C., August 29, 2024  \u2013 Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced the 30 community foundations, serving communities in 10 Appalachian states, selected to participate in   READY Community Foundations  . One of four tailored tracks under ARC\u2019s capacity-building initiative,   READY Appalachia  ,  READY Community Foundations  provides no-cost training and funding access to help Appalachia\u2019s local philanthropic organizations improve their programming, operations, fundraising and financial impact. \n \n\n \n The  30 organizations  selected for  READY Community Foundations  will participate in six weeks of virtual, cohort-based courses designed to improve their skills in financial management, endowment management, community-rooted fundraising and grantmaking, mission and strategy development and more. \n \n\n \n \u201cARC remains committed to providing our Appalachian communities with the resources they need to build toward a prosperous future, including training to strengthen community foundation capacity,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cThrough the training provided by  READY Community Foundations , Appalachia\u2019s philanthropic organizations will be better able to respond to community needs and foster economic development in the areas they serve.\u201d \n \n\n \n Following completion of the training program, participants will be eligible to apply for up to $25,000 in funding (no match required) to implement internal capacity-building projects that will help them better serve Appalachian Kentucky, Maryland, Mississippi, New York, North Carolina, Ohio, Pennsylvania, Tennessee, Virginia, and West Virginia. \n \n\n \n All of the  READY Community Foundations  participants serve at least one of the following: \n \n\n \n \n Distressed areas and census tracts \n\n\n\n Interagency Working Group on Coal and Power Plant Communities  priority communities  \n\n\n\n Rural Partners Network  Community Networks communities  \n\n\n\n Historically underserved populations \n \n \n\n \n Supported with funding from the Bipartisan Infrastructure Law,  READY Community Foundations  in the fourth and final track under our capacity-building initiative, READY Appalachia. Since the launch of READY Appalachia in July 2022, ARC has provided training and access to funding to 41  local development districts  (LDDs), 75 nonprofits, and 90  local government  entities to help them build internal capacity and better serve their communities. To date, nearly $4 million has been awarded through   READY Local Development Districts   and more than $1.6 million has been awarded through   READY Nonprofits  . \n \n\n \n \u201cToday\u2019s  READY Community Foundations  announcement is yet another example of how the Biden-Harris Administration\u2019s Bipartisan Infrastructure Law is revitalizing the foundations of local communities across America \u2013 including energy communities that have historically been left behind in Appalachia,\u201d said  National Economic Advisor Lael Brainard.  \n \n\n \n To learn more about the 30  READY Community Foundations  participants, visit  https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/ready\/foundations\/ . \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission  \n \n\n \n The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. \n \n\n \n  About READY Appalachia  \n \n\n \n  READY Appalachia  is an ARC community-capacity building initiative for nonprofit organizations, local development districts (LDDs), local governments, and community foundations serving the Appalachian Region. Participants access no-cost training, cohort-based learning, and funding opportunities (no match required) to increase their ability to solve pressing issues and create lasting, positive change in communities. \n ","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":" READY Community Foundations will provide 30 community foundations serving the Appalachian Region with no-cost training and access to funding to expand local philanthropic impact. WASHINGTON, D.C., August 29, 2024 \u2013 Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced the 30 community foundations, serving communities in 10 Appalachian states, selected to participate in READY Community Foundations. Administration Designates Nine Tech Hubs and Grant Recipients in Appalachian Region"},"content":{"rendered":" \n   As part of the President\u2019s Investing in America agenda, the U.S. Department of Commerce identified regional centers primed for technological innovation and job creation.   \n \n\n \n WASHINGTON, DC \u2014 The Biden-Harris administration, through the U.S. Department of Commerce\u2019s Economic Development Administration (EDA),  announced  the designation of 31 Tech Hubs in regions across the country\u2014three of which are in in the Appalachian Region. EDA also awarded six Strategy Development Grants (SDG) to Appalachian entities to help communities significantly increase local coordination and planning activities. Such development could make selected grantees more competitive for future Tech Hubs funding opportunities.
This is the first phase of the new  Tech Hubs program , which is an economic development initiative designed to drive regional innovation and job creation by strengthening a region\u2019s capacity to manufacture, commercialize, and deploy technology that will advance American competitiveness. The program invests directly in burgeoning, high-potential U.S. regions and aims to transform them into globally competitive innovation centers. \n \n\n \n \u201cI\u2019m so appreciative of the EDA for recognizing the skillset of our Appalachian workforce, and for their continuing willingness to keep in mind the priority service areas of their fellow federal entities,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cThe creation of new Appalachian tech hubs not only emphasizes our region\u2019s growth, but also the ways in which we are being connected with the rest of the country through emerging industries.\u201d \n \n\n \n  The Appalachian Tech Hubs are:  \n \n\n \n \n  Birmingham Biotechnology Hub  \u2013 equitable AI-driven biotechnology in AL \n\n\n\n  SC Nexus for Advanced Resilient Energy  \u2013 clean energy supply chain in SC and GA \n\n\n\n  New Energy New York (NENY) Battery Tech Hub  \u2013 end to end battery development and manufacturing in NY \n \n \n\n \n  The Appalachian Strategy Development Grant (SDG) Recipients are:  \n \n\n \n \n  North Central Pressed Materials Strategy Development Consortium  \n\n\n\n  Southeast Biotech Collaborative (SEBC) Strategy Development Consortium  \n\n\n\n  Virginia’s Additive Manufacturing & Applied Materials Strategy Development Consortium  \n\n\n\n  West Virginia Advanced Energy & Industrial Technology Manufacturing (WV-AEIM) Strategy Development Consortium  \n\n\n\n  Western North Carolina Industrialized Construction (NCIC) Strategy Development Consortium  \n\n\n\n  WV Tech Strategy Development Consortium  \n \n \n\n \n Tech Hubs was authorized by the bipartisan CHIPS and Science Act, a key part of President Biden\u2019s Investing in America agenda, which he signed into law in August 2022.   \n \n\n \n These Tech Hubs represent a cross-section of urban and rural regions. Designation is an endorsement of the region\u2019s plans to supercharge their respective technological industries to create jobs, strengthen U.S. competitiveness, and protect national security. The Tech Hubs announced focus on industries ranging from autonomous systems, quantum computing, biotechnology, precision medicine, clean energy advancement, semiconductor manufacturing, and more, and highlight how the Biden Harris Administration is investing in innovation and economic growth in every region of the United States. \n \n\n \n \u201cThese outstanding Tech Hubs Designees exemplify place-based economic development strategies at their best: combining federal resources with regional assets, expertise, and coalitions to implement transformational opportunities, said  Assistant Secretary of Commerce for Economic Development Alejandra Y. Castillo . \u201cAs each region develops its own strategy to catalyze innovation and job creation, the entire nation grows more secure and more competitive.\u201d \n \n\n \n  Read more about the Tech Hubs program at    TechHubs.gov  . \n \n\n \n Read the full EDA press release here:  https:\/\/www.eda.gov\/news\/press-release\/2023\/10\/23\/biden-harris-administration-designates-31-tech-hubs-across-america  \n \n\n \n  About the U.S. Economic Development Administration (   www.eda.gov   )
The mission of the U.S. Economic Development Administration (EDA) is to lead the federal economic development agenda by promoting competitiveness and preparing the nation\u2019s regions for growth and success in the worldwide economy. An agency within the U.S. Department of Commerce, EDA invests in communities and supports regional collaboration in order to create jobs for U.S. workers, promote American innovation, and accelerate long-term sustainable economic growth. \n ","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":" As part of the President\u2019s Investing in America agenda, the U.S. Department of Commerce identified regional centers primed for technological innovation and job creation. WASHINGTON, DC \u2014 The Biden-Harris administration, through the U.S. Department of Commerce\u2019s Economic Development Administration (EDA), announced the designation of 31 Tech Hubs in regions across the country\u2014three of which are […] rc.gov\/wp-json\/wp\/v2\/operational_guidance_type?post=41209"}],"curies":[{"name":"wp","href":"https:\/\/api.w.org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}},{"id":40957,"date":"2023-10-10T08:52:47","date_gmt":"2023-10-10T13:52:47","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/?p=40957"},"modified":"2025-05-27T09:44:49","modified_gmt":"2025-05-27T13:44:49","slug":"biden-harris-administration-through-arc-awards-14-5-million-to-grow-manufacturing-in-green-energy-sectors-workforce-development-opportunities-in-northern-and-central-appalachia","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/biden-harris-administration-through-arc-awards-14-5-million-to-grow-manufacturing-in-green-energy-sectors-workforce-development-opportunities-in-northern-and-central-appalachia\/","title":{"rendered":"ARC Awards $14.5M+ to Grow Manufacturing in Northern and Central Appalachia"},"content":{"rendered":" \n   Two new ARISE grants strengthen manufacturing and energy production and prepare youth for in-demand jobs across five states.    \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C., October 10, 2023 \u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) awarded two new grants totaling over $14.5 million to boost energy manufacturing and workforce development through its Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE) funding opportunity, which drives large-scale economic transformation through multi-state collaboration:  \n \n\n \n \n  A $10 million ARISE grant to Catalyst Connection of Pittsburgh, PA  will help 1,000+ small to medium manufacturers expand or enter emerging technology and energy sectors in 156 counties across Appalachian Pennsylvania, Maryland, New York, Ohio and West Virginia.  Funds will be used to provide training, technical assistance, supply chain mapping, and guidance on factory upgrades. \n\n\n\n  A $4.5+ million ARISE grant to Northern Tier Regional Planning and Development Commission of Towanda, PA  will prepare and train an estimated 3,400+ students and young adults in Pennsylvania and New York for jobs in emerging industries. The project aims to engage employers with youth education providers to increase labor participation in Northern Appalachia.  \n \n \n\n \n \u201cARC\u2019s ARISE grants are making transformational, cross-boundary impacts on our Appalachian states,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cThe work of our newest ARISE grantees will help boost workforce opportunities across the region by providing integral job training in growing career fields, which in turn will allow Appalachia to compete economically on a national and global level.\u201d\u00a0 \n \n\n \n The projects are funded through  ARISE , ARC\u2019s initiative to drive large-scale, regional economic transformation through multi-state collaborative projects across Appalachia. ARC launched ARISE in August of 2022 to strengthen Appalachian business and industry, and to grow and support the development of new economic opportunities across multiple states. A total of $227 million has been made available through ARISE to date. \n \n\n \n \u201cIn Kentucky, and across Appalachia, we\u2019re seeing the growth of new industries, bringing jobs an opportunity to our communities,\u201d said  ARC 2023 States\u2019 Co-Chair, Kentucky Governor Andy Beshear . \u201cThese ARISE funds are helping ensure we have enough workers to meet the demands of this economic growth. This initiative is a great example of working across state lines to benefit our region and build a brighter future for all of our people.\u201d  \n \n\n \n \u201cPresident Biden\u2019s Investing in America agenda is growing the economy from the bottom up and middle out, bringing good-paying jobs to communities that have too long been overlooked,\u201d said  Mitch Landrieu, Senior Advisor to the President and White House Infrastructure Coordinator . \u201cThese new ARISE awards will expand opportunity, boost domestic manufacturing, and bring the benefits of a clean energy economy to communities across Northern and Central Appalachia.\u201d\u00a0 \n \n\n \n Learn more about ARISE and the recent updates to the application process:  www.arc.gov\/ARISE .  \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The Appalachian Regional Commission is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. \n Announces Appalachian Leadership Institute Fellows for 2023-2024\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":" \n   New class of 40 fellows represents public, private, and nonprofit sectors across Appalachia\u2019s 13 states    \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C., September 26, 2023 \u2014Today, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) announced the 40 fellows selected for the 2023-2024 Class of the  Appalachian Leadership Institute , a free leadership and economic development training opportunity for individuals currently living and\/or working in one of ARC\u2019s  13 Appalachian states . The 2023-2024 class comprises a diverse network of professionals representing all 13 Appalachian states and a wide spectrum of perspectives and sectors, including tourism, healthcare, education, civil service, and more.\u00a0\u00a0 \n \n\n \n The nine-month curriculum is anchored by six multi-day seminars across the Appalachian Region focusing on each of ARC\u2019s  strategic investment priorities , which aim to strengthen economic and community growth in Appalachia. From October 2023 through July 2024, this class will focus on skill-building and best practice reviews to prepare fellows to:  \n \n\n \n \n Design effective economic development project proposals \n\n\n\n Integrate community assets into long-term economic development strategies \n\n\n\n Identify resources available to spark economic and community development \n\n\n\n Locate and access investment capital from a variety of public and private sources \n\n\n\n Prepare competitive applications for public grant opportunities \n\n\n\n Use expanded connections and leadership skills to create strong regional partnerships \n \n \n\n \n \u201cGrowing and supporting the next generation of Appalachian leaders is integral to the future success of our region,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cI send my sincerest congratulations to the newest round of Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows, as well as my appreciation for all the work and passion they put into helping their communities thrive and compete.\u201d  \n \n\n \n The fifth class of Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows were selected via a competitive application process to reflect the Appalachian region\u2019s wide range of economic development challenges, opportunities, and strategies. The 2023-2024 Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows are:   \n \n\n \n  Alabama   \n \n\n \n \n  Kenneth Anderson , Director, City of Huntsville \u2013 DEI, Madison  \n \n \n\n \n \n  Paul Kennedy , President, Walker Area Community Foundation, Jasper  \n\n\n\n  Jesslan Wilson , Director, Regional Planning Commission of Greater Birmingham, Trussville  \n \n \n\n \n  Georgia   \n \n\n \n \n  Jason Mock , President\/CEO, Greater Dalton Chamber of Commerce, Dalton  \n\n\n\n  Pam Powers-Smith , President, Rome Floyd Chamber of Commerce, Rome  \n \n \n\n \n  Kentucky   \n \n\n \n \n  Jordan Campbell , Executive Director, Gateway Regional Arts Center, Mount Sterling  \n\n\n\n  Eric Dixon , Senior Researcher, Ohio River Valley Institute, Louisville  \n\n\n\n  Jacqueline Kohl-Hamilton , Executive Director & Senior Lecturer, Why We Write, Inc., Eastern Kentucky University, Winchester  \n\n\n\n  Candace Tingen , Copywriter, Pineville  \n \n \n\n \n  Maryland   \n \n\n \n \n  Kathy Beachler , Executive Director, Garrett County Arts Council, Oakland  \n\n\n\n  Stephanie Hutter-Thomas , Founder\/CEO, Appalachian Recovery Concepts, LLC & WVU School of Medicine, Cumberland   \n \n Executive Director, Broome County Council of Churches, Binghamton   \n \n \n\n \n  North Carolina   \n \n\n \n \n  Amanda Hall , Executive Director, Center for Community Engagement and Service Learning, Western Carolina University, Whittier  \n\n\n\n  Justin Kearley , Rural Health Operations Specialist, NC Office of Rural Health, NC DHHS, Boone  \n\n\n\n  Michael Pesant , Community Investment Officer, Dogwood Health Trust, Asheville  \n \n \n\n \n  Ohio   \n \n\n \n \n  Amanda Crabtree , Marketing Director, Vinton County National Bank, Jackson  \n\n\n\n  Chris Schaffer , Director of Grants and Sponsored Research, Shawnee State University, Waverly  \n\n\n\n  Bailey Venzin , Executive Director, Hocking Hills Chamber of Commerce, Logan  \n \n \n\n \n  Pennsylvania   \n \n\n \n \n  Amy Camp , Founder, Cycle Forward, Pittsburgh  \n\n\n\n  Elizabeth Lockwood , Director, Project Development\/Grants Economic Development Program, SEDA- Council of Governments, Lewisburg  \n\n\n\n  Jamelle Price , External Relations Manager, University of Pittsburgh, Bethel Park  \n\n\n\n  Daniel Rossi-Keen , Executive Director, RiverWise, Aliquippa  \n \n \n\n \n  South Carolina   \n Knoxville  \n \n \n\n \n  Virginia   \n \n\n \n \n  Mary Newlyn , Executive Director, WV Hope in Action Alliance, Winchester  \n\n\n\n  Annette Patterson , President, The Advancement Foundation, Director, McDowell County Convention and Visitors Bureau, Welch  \n\n\n\n  Jennifer Light , Program Director of Industrial Technologies, Allegany College of Maryland, Ridgeley  \n\n\n\n  Kalyn Obiozor-Dorey , Community Coaching Associate, West Virginia Community Development Hub, Huntington  \n \n \n\n \n \n  Jona Rinard , Dean of Transfer and Public Service, Washington State Community College, Williamstown  \n \n \n\n \n Upon completion of the program, fellows are automatically part of the Appalachian Leadership Institute Alumni Network, a peer-to-peer working group of experts helping foster collaborative solutions that will build a stronger future for Appalachia.  \n \n\n \n \u201cThe Appalachian Leadership Institute supports leaders already doing amazing work across Eastern Kentucky and the entire Appalachian region,\u201d said  ARC 2023 States\u2019 Co-Chair, Kentucky Governor Andy Beshear . \u201cThis program capitalizes on the incredibly talented people who are already invested in these special communities. When we invest in our people, we\u2019re building a brighter future for generations to come.\u201d  \n \n\n \n To learn more about the program, visit:  arc.gov\/leadership . To meet the 2023-2024 class of fellows and learn more about how they are working to transform their Appalachian communities,  visit this link .  \n \n\n \n  \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Leadership Institute
The Appalachian Leadership Institute is a comprehensive regional leadership training program developed by the Appalachian Regional Commission in partnership with the University of Tennessee, Knoxville; The Howard H. Baker School of Public Policy and Public Affairs; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact. Learn more at  www.arc.gov\/leadership .   \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The  Appalachian Regional Commission  is an economic development entity of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.  \n Awards $6.3 Million to Bolster Broadband Access Across 50 Communities in Every Appalachian Subregion\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":" \n  The first-ever grant funded through ARC\u2019s new Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE), led by Connect Humanity, will provide support to under-connected communities throughout Appalachia, better positioning them to grow broadband economic opportunities.  \n \n\n \n  \n  \n  \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C., January 11, 2023 \u2014Today, ARC awarded $6.3 million through its new   Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE)   funding opportunity to  Connect Humanity  for a project that will help 50 underserved communities in  every subregion  of Appalachia plan for broadband access and growth.    \n \n\n \n Working with local partners in 12 Appalachian states\u2014Georgia, Kentucky, Maryland, Mississippi, New York, North Carolina, Ohio, Pennsylvania, South Carolina, Tennessee, Virginia, and West Virginia\u2014Connect Humanity will help communities develop tailored digital equity plans designed to deliver appropriate technology, business, and financing plans necessary to ensure high-speed and affordable internet for all, device access, and digital literacy training.\u202f The growing list of project partners include:  \n \n\n \n   Center on Rural Innovation     Empire State Development     Enterprise Center     Fair Count     Generation WV     Mississippi Alliance of Nonprofits & Philanthropy     NC State University Institute for Emerging Issues     SOAR \u2013 Shaping Our Appalachian Region     Student Freedom Initiative     Center for Strategic Partnerships at the Pennsylvania Department of Community & Economic Development     Virginia Funders Network    \n \n\n \n While a central focus of the project will be to help 50 under-connected communities across Appalachia compete for some of the $45 billion from the  Broadband Equity, Access, and Deployment (BEAD)  and  Digital Equity Act (DEA)  funding that will become available later in 2023, communities will also benefit from the technical assistance provided as part of the project to put them on a path to digital equity. \n \n\n \n \u201cARC\u2019s first ARISE grant to Connect Humanity has tremendous potential to drive large-scale, regional transformation around broadband access,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cBroadband access is essential for Appalachia to thrive and compete in a global economy; without this support, our most rural communities may be left further behind. I commend our states and community partners in every Appalachian subregion for coming together in order to fully participate in our digitalized world.\u201d \n \n\n \n \u201cPresident Biden\u2019s Bipartisan infrastructure Law is making sure we don\u2019t leave any community behind as we grow the economy from the bottom up and middle out, especially Appalachia,\u201d said  Mitch Landrieu, Senior Advisor to the President and White House Infrastructure Coordinator.  \u201cUnder the leadership of Co-Chair Gayle Manchin, ARC is providing critical resources to make sure states are prepared to help connect all of their residents to affordable, reliable high-speed internet.\u201d \n \n\n \n \u201cAccess to affordable, high-speed internet is something every family across Appalachia needs and deserves. This project is another important step to ensuring broadband reaches every American,\u201d said  ARC States\u2019 Co-Chair Governor Andy Beshear of Kentucky .  \n \n\n \n \u201cWith the U.S. government about to make its biggest ever investment to close the digital divide, it\u2019s critical that the communities that most need support to build better broadband can secure these funds,\u201d said  Connect Humanity Program Lead Chris Worman , who added, \u201cThis program will put 50 communities in Appalachia on the path to getting fast, reliable, affordable internet, serving hundreds of thousands of families and businesses with the connectivity they need to thrive in an increasingly digital world.\u201d  \n \n\n \n Appalachian communities can learn more and apply at  connecthumanity.fund\/arise . \n \n\n \n  ARC\u2019s ARISE Funding Opportunity
ARISE  is ARC\u2019s newest initiative to drive large-scale, regional economic transformation through multi-state collaborative projects across Appalachia. Making available $73.5 million in FY 2022 provided through the Bipartisan Infrastructure Law, also known as the   Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act of 2021  , ARC launched ARISE in August of 2022 to strengthen Appalachian business and industry, and to grow and support the development of new economic opportunities across multiple states. Learn more:  arc.gov\/ARISE . \n \n\n \n  \n \n\n \n   About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The  Appalachian Regional Commission  is an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC’s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation. \n ","protected":false},"excerpt":{"rendered":" The first-ever grant funded through ARC\u2019s new Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE), led by Connect Humanity, will provide support to under-connected communities throughout Appalachia, better positioning them to grow broadband economic opportunities. WASHINGTON, D.C., January 11, 2023\u2014Today, ARC awarded $6.3 million through its new Appalachian Regional Initiative for Stronger Economies (ARISE) funding opportunity […] Regional Commission (ARC) Hosts 160 Appalachian College and University Students at Community Research Symposium\u00a0"},"content":{"rendered":" \n   Beginning today, students from 16 colleges and universities in 10 Appalachian states will share capstone research at the 22 nd  annual Appalachian Teaching Project symposium.    \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C., December 2, 2022  \u2014 The Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) is hosting 160\u202fstudents from 16\u202fAppalachian colleges and universities for the 22 nd  annual  Appalachian Teaching Project (ATP)  Symposium at the Renaissance Arlington Capital View in Arlington, Virginia, today, Friday, December 2, and Saturday, December 3, 2022.    \n \n\n \n Supported by ARC and organized by the\u202fCenter for Appalachian Studies and Services\u202fat  East Tennessee State University (ETSU) , participating schools offer a for-credit seminar during the fall semester to help students develop and execute field-based research projects that address the unique economic development needs of their communities and align with ARC\u2019s investment priorities.  \n \n\n \n As a capstone, students will present their work to other student delegations from participating institutions, ARC leadership, and community leaders in a formal peer-to-peer conference setting hosted by ARC and the Consortium of Appalachian Colleges and Universities.  \n \n\n \n \u201cYoung leaders, such as the students who participated in this year\u2019s Appalachian Teaching Project, are integral to shaping the future of economic development throughout Appalachia,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cI\u2019m impressed by the hard work they put into their research projects and look forward to following their journeys as they continue to positively impact their communities, and communities across our region.\u201d  \n \n\n \n Student delegations from the following schools participating in this year\u2019s ATP Symposium include:\u202f   \n \n\n \n  Alfred State College,  Alfred, New York   \n \n\n \n  Appalachian State University ,\u202fBoone, North Carolina\u202f   \n \n\n \n  Auburn University , Auburn, Alabama\u202f   \n \n\n \n  East Tennessee State University , Johnson City, Tennessee\u202f   \n \n\n \n  Frostburg State University,  Frostburg, Maryland   \n \n\n \n  Indiana University of Pennsylvania , Indiana, Pennsylvania\u202f   \n \n\n \n  Morehead State University , Morehead, Kentucky\u202f   \n \n\n \n  Muskingum University,  New Concord, Ohio   \n \n\n \n  Ohio University , Athens, Ohio\u202f      \n \n\n \n  Radford University , Radford, Virginia\u202f   \n \n\n \n  University of North Alabama , Florence, Alabama\u202f   \n \n\n \n  University of Pittsburgh , Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania\u202f   \n \n\n \n  University of Pittsburgh \u2013 Bradford , Bradford, Pennsylvania\u202f   \n \n\n \n  University of Tennessee , Knoxville, Tennessee\u202f   \n \n\n \n  Virginia Tech,\u202f Blacksburg, Virginia\u202f   \n \n\n \n  West Liberty University,  West Liberty, West Virginia   \n \n\n \n Summaries of each research project are available at\u202fon ARC\u2019s   website   .   \n \n\n \n Since 2001, more than\u202f2,800\u202fcollege and graduate students from 22 colleges and universities across Appalachia have participated in the Appalachian Teaching Project. During the symposium, ARC and ETSU will announce that ATP is being renamed to the  Appalachian Collegiate Research Initiative (ACRI)  to better reflect the valuable research participants prepare for communities. ARC has  released an RFP  seeking college and university applicants for ACRI in 2023.  Applications are due March 31, 2023.   \n \n\n \n ACRI is one of ARC\u2019s four\u202f academies and institutes,  offering experiential learning opportunities where participants build collaborative networks, hone skills, and foster an enduring commitment to Appalachia\u2019s future.\u202f   \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The\u202f Appalachian Regional Commission \u202fis an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.  \n Announces Appalachian Leadership Institute Fellows for 2022-2023"},"content":{"rendered":" \n   New class of 40 fellows represents public, private, and nonprofit sectors across Appalachia\u2019s 13 states   \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C., October 6, 2022 \u2014Today the  Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC)  announced the 40 fellows selected for the 2022-2023 Class of the  Appalachian Leadership Institute , a free leadership and economic development training opportunity for individuals currently living and\/or working in one of ARC\u2019s  13 Appalachian states . The 2022-2023 class comprises a diverse network of professionals representing all 13 Appalachian states and a wide spectrum of perspectives and sectors, including tourism, healthcare, education, civil service, and more. \n \n\n \n The nine-month curriculum is anchored by six multi-day seminars across the Appalachian region focusing on each of ARC\u2019s  strategic investment priorities , which aim to strengthen economic and community growth in Appalachia. The fourth class will run from October 2022 through July 2023, focusing on skill-building seminars and best practice reviews to prepare fellows to: \n \n\n \n \n Design effective economic development project proposals \n\n\n\n Integrate community assets into long-term economic development strategies \n\n\n\n Identify resources available to spark economic and community development \n\n\n\n Locate and access investment capital from a variety of public and private sources \n\n\n\n Prepare competitive applications for public grant opportunities \n\n\n\n Use expanded connections and leadership skills to create strong regional partnerships \n \n \n\n \n “Congratulations to the incoming 2022-2023 class of Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows! These leaders are already growing their Appalachian communities and will be even better equipped to drive positive change after their work with this program,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cI am eager to see this class of fellows collaborate across state lines to set big goals that will help the entire Appalachian region thrive.\u201d \n \n\n \n Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows were selected via a competitive application process to reflect the Appalachian region\u2019s wide range of economic development challenges, opportunities, and strategies. The 2022-2023 Appalachian Leadership Institute fellows are: \n \n\n \n  Alabama  \n \n\n \n \n  Gary Padgett,  Professor, University of North Alabama, Florence \n\n\n\n  Jenny Stubbs,  Owner, Yellow House Publishing, Wetumpka \n \n \n\n \n  Georgia  \n \n\n \n \n  Tee Faircloth,  Founder\/CEO, Coordinated Care Inc., Atlanta \n\n\n\n  Bobbi Larson,  Director of Economic Development and Community Engagement, University of North Georgia, Dahlonega \n \n \n\n \n  Kentucky  \n \n\n \n \n  Baylen Campbell,  Director of Community Impact, Invest Appalachia, Hazard \n\n\n\n  Joanie Jasper,  Executive Director, Middlesboro Main Street, Middlesboro \n\n\n\n  Tammie Nazario,  President\/CEO, Eastern Kentucky PRIDE, Somerset \n\n\n\n  Aaron Poynter,  Director of Reentry Programming, Cumberlands and South Central Workforce Development Boards, Russell Springs \n\n\n\n  Will Warren , Economic Development Consultant, Vita Nuova, London \n \n \n\n \n  Maryland  \n \n\n \n \n  Jim Raley,  Executive Director, Archway Station, Cumberland \n\n\n\n  David Shuster,  President\/CEO, Horizon Goodwill Industries, Hagerstown \n \n \n\n \n  Mississippi  \n \n\n \n \n  Kristy Luse,  Vice President, Toyota Schoharie County \/ Schoharie County Historical Society, Schoharie \n\n\n\n  Bea Nellenback,  Development Director, Alternatives Federal Credit Union, Ithaca \n\n\n\n  Martha Ryan , Public and School Birney , Project Manager, Sunday Creek Horizons, Athens \n\n\n\n  Sam Miller , Development Director, Buckeye Hills Regional Council, Marietta \n\n\n\n  Tasha Werry , Executive Director, BB2C, Marietta \n \n \n\n \n  Pennsylvania  \n \n\n \n \n  Amanda Craig Bradley , Business Outreach Manager, DRIVE, Danville \n\n\n\n  Holly Pilcavage , President\/CEO, Coal Creative, Wilkes-Barre \n\n\n\n  Todd Pousley , Neighborhood Revitalization Manager, NeighborWorks Northeastern Pennsylvania, Scranton \n \n \n\n \n  South Carolina  \n \n\n \n \n  Mackenzie Chastain , Probation Supervisor, South Carolina Department of Juvenile Justice, Anderson \n\n\n\n  Allison Fowler , Director of Community and Tourism Development, Pickens County, Pickens \n\n\n\n  Kyle Sox , Vice President of Industrial To meet the 2022-2023 class of fellows and learn more about how they are working to transform their Appalachian communities, visit:  arc.gov\/appalachian-leadership-institute-class\/ . \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Leadership Institute
The Appalachian Leadership Institute is a comprehensive regional leadership training program developed by the Appalachian Regional Commission in partnership with the University of Tennessee, Knoxville; The Howard H. Baker Center for Public Policy; Tuskegee University; and Collective Impact. Regional Commission to Launch Virtual \u201cRoadshow\u201d Series to Showcase Impact of Strategic Investments in Appalachian Communities"},"content":{"rendered":" \n  Launching March 1, the Virtual Event Series Will Feature Governors, Community Members, and Economic Development Professionals Highlighting Workforce Development  \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C., February 10, 2022 \u2014Following the reveal of its new 2022\u20132026 strategic plan, \u201cAppalachia Envisioned,\u201d the  Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC)  will embark on a virtual event series to highlight how ARC\u2019s investments are transforming Appalachian communities in each of its 13 states. The virtual event series (titled the  Appalachia Envisioned Roadshow ) will showcase the voices and experiences of Appalachian communities leveraging ARC grant funding to strengthen and fuel economic growth in the region. \n \n\n \n Each roadshow event will spotlight one of ARC\u2019s five strategic investment priorities and feature voices of governors, community members, and economic development professionals from ARC partner states.  Stop 1: Building Workforce Ecosystems in Maryland, New York and Pennsylvania , the first event of the series, will take place on  Tuesday, March 1  from 9:30-11:30 am EST . ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin will facilitate an engaging discussion with ARC States\u2019 Co-Chair Governor Larry Hogan of Maryland on the challenges and successes in creating and expanding workforce ecosystems in his state. Community members will also participate in a panel to share their experiences from the frontlines of bringing ARC\u2019s investment priority of building workforce ecosystems to life. \n \n\n \n \u201cThe future of Appalachia is bright because of the individuals in the region actively engaged in efforts to strengthen and elevate their communities each day,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cThe  Appalachia Envisioned Roadshow  is a five-part virtual event series, which will spotlight the voices and experiences of leaders and communities in all 13 states, while facilitating conversations about resilience, innovation, sustainability, and equity through the lens of ARC\u2019s new strategic plan. I look forward to kicking off the series with our partners in Maryland, Pennsylvania, and New York, as well as collaborating with additional state partners for future events.\u201d \n \n\n \n \u201cEach day, communities across Appalachia are transformed through investments made by the ARC,\u201d said  ARC States\u2019 Co-Chair Governor Larry Hogan.  \u201cThere is no better way to further amplify and strengthen our mission than to spotlight the success stories of leaders and communities throughout the region.\u201d \n \n\n \n Dates and times are forthcoming for the remaining four roadshow events, which will focus on the following ARC strategic investment priorities: \n \n\n \n \n Building Appalachian Business \n\n\n\n Building Appalachia’s Infrastructure \n\n\n\n Building Regional Culture and Tourism \n\n\n\n Building Community Leaders and Capacity  \n \n \n\n \n Learn more and register for  Stop #1: Building Workforce Ecosystems with Maryland, New York, and Pennsylvania.  To receive the latest information about the remaining  Appalachia Envisioned Roadshow  stops, sign up for ARC\u2019s   In the Region  newsletter . \n \n\n \n  \n  \n  \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The  Appalachian Regional Commission  is an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.   \n w.org\/{rel}","templated":true}]}},{"id":31578,"date":"2021-11-30T11:00:00","date_gmt":"2021-11-30T16:00:00","guid":{"rendered":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/?p=31578"},"modified":"2021-11-30T11:08:34","modified_gmt":"2021-11-30T16:08:34","slug":"arc-hosts-21st-annual-appalachian-teaching-project-atp-featuring-economic-development-research-conducted-by-regions-college-and-university-students","status":"publish","type":"post","link":"https:\/\/www.arc.gov\/news\/arc-hosts-21st-annual-appalachian-teaching-project-atp-featuring-economic-development-research-conducted-by-regions-college-and-university-students\/","title":{"rendered":"ARC Hosts 21st Annual Appalachian Teaching Project (ATP) Featuring Economic Development Research Conducted by Region\u2019s College and University Students"},"content":{"rendered":" \n  150 Students from 15 Colleges and Universities in 10 Appalachian States to Participate in Online Symposium   \n \n\n \n  WASHINGTON, D.C. ,  November 30, 2021 \u2014This week, from Tuesday, November 30 to Friday, December 3, 2021, the Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) will host 150 students from 15 Appalachian based colleges and universities at the 21st annual\u202f Appalachian Teaching Project (ATP) Conference .   \n \n\n \n ATP is an applied-research academic program training college and graduate students to design community based economic development initiatives across the Appalachian Region. As part of ATP, students enroll in a for-credit course and conduct field research to identify and solve a community development issue during the fall semester. As a capstone to this work, students present their work to other student delegations from ATP participating institutions, ARC leadership, and community leaders in a formal peer-to-peer conference setting hosted by ARC. Normally, this conference is held in Washington, D.C. where students use the trip as an opportunity to meet with policy leaders and learn more about the city. However, due to the COVID-19 pandemic, the ATP Symposium is being held online again this year.  \n \n\n \n \u201cAppalachia needs young leaders, like those participating in the Appalachian Teaching Project, to bring forward-thinking economic development plans to life,\u201d said  ARC Federal Co-Chair Gayle Manchin . \u201cThis group of change-makers is capable of creating positive impacts in our communities, and I am excited to see the lasting effects these innovative projects will have on the Region long-term.\u201d\u00a0 \n \n\n \n Student delegations from the following schools participating in this year\u2019s ATP will give presentations focusing on addressing community needs and challenges, economic development, tourism, and capacity building:  \n \n\n \n  Alfred State College , Alfred, New York  \n \n\n \n  Appalachian State University , Boone, North Carolina  \n \n\n \n  Auburn University , Auburn, Alabama  \n \n\n \n  East Tennessee State University , Johnson City, Tennessee  \n \n\n \n  Fairmont State University , Fairmont, West Virginia  \n \n\n \n  Indiana University of Pennsylvania , Indiana, Pennsylvania  \n \n\n \n  Ohio University , Athens, Ohio  \n \n\n \n  Morehead State University , Morehead, Kentucky  \n \n\n \n  Radford University , Radford, Virginia  \n \n\n \n  University of North Alabama , Florence, Alabama  \n \n\n \n  University of Pittsburgh , Pittsburgh, Pennsylvania  \n \n\n \n  University of Pittsburgh – Bradford , Bradford, Pennsylvania  \n \n\n \n  University of Tennessee , Knoxville, Tennessee  \n \n\n \n  Virginia Tech,  Blacksburg, Virginia  \n \n\n \n  Young Harris College , Young Harris, Georgia  \n \n\n \n Since 2001, over 2,650 college and graduate students from across Appalachia have participated in the Appalachian Teaching Project. Many ATP alumnae have gone on to careers in public service, community development, and other related fields across the Region. Summaries of each research project are available at  www.arc.gov\/atp . ATP is one of four  academies and institutes  sponsored by the Appalachian Regional Commission. Through these experiential learning opportunities, participants build networks, hone skills, and cultivate an enduring commitment to Appalachia\u2019s future.  \n \n\n \n Supported by ARC and organized by the\u202f Center for Appalachian Studies and Services \u202fat East Tennessee State University (ETSU), students receive college credit for participating in ATP, and schools participating in ATP offer a directed seminar guiding students in developing and executing field-based research projects specific to the needs of their surrounding communities and in alignment with ARC\u2019s  investment priorities .  \n \n\n \n  About the Appalachian Regional Commission
The  Appalachian Regional Commission  is an economic development agency of the federal government and 13 state   governments focusing on 423 counties across the Appalachian Region. ARC\u2019s mission is to innovate, partner, and invest to build community capacity and strengthen economic growth in Appalachia to help the Region achieve socioeconomic parity with the nation.  \n
